Tollmien-Schlichting waves in the subsonic regime
Nader Masmoudi, Yuxi Wang, Di Wu, Zhifei Zhang

Abstract
. The Tollmien-Schlichting (T-S) waves play a key role in the early stages of boundary layer transition. In a breakthrough work [ 13 ] , Grenier, Guo and Nguyen gave the first rigorous construction of the T-S waves of temporal mode for the incompressible fluid. Yang and Zhang [ 27 ] recently made an important contribution by constructing the compressible T-S waves of temporal mode for certain boundary layer profiles with Mach number m < 1 3 m<\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} . In this paper, we construct the T-S waves of both temporal mode and spatial mode to the linearized compressible Navier-Stokes system around the boundary layer flow in the whole subsonic regime m < 1 m<1 , including the Blasius profile. Our approach is based on a novel iteration scheme between the quasi-incompressible and quasi-compressible systems, with a key ingredient being the solution of an Orr-Sommerfeld type equation using a new Airy-Airy-Rayleigh iteration instead of Rayleigh-Airy iteration introduced by Grenier, Guo and Nguyen. We believe the method developed in this work can be applied in solving other related problems for subsonic flows.



1.
Introduction

In this paper, we are concerned with the stability problem for an isentropic compressible flow over a flat plate at high Reynolds number regime
Re\gg 1
. The motion of the fluid is described by the non-dimensional compressible Navier-Stokes system

(1.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\rho^{\nu}+\nabla\cdot(\rho^{\nu}U^{\nu})=0,\\
&\rho^{\nu}\big(\partial_{t}U^{\nu}+U^{\nu}\cdot\nabla U^{\nu}\big)-\mu\nu\Delta U^{\nu}-\lambda\nu\nabla\nabla\cdot U^{\nu}+\nabla P(\rho^{\nu})=0,\\
&U^{\nu}|_{y=0}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Here
\rho^{\nu}
is the density,
U^{\nu}
is the velocity, and
P(\rho^{\nu})
is the pressure. Here
\mu,\lambda
are the viscosity coefficients and
\nu>0
is propositional to
Re^{-1}
.

At high Reynolds number, the behavior of the flow away from the boundary can be described by the Euler equations and the behavior near the boundary can be described by the Prandtl equation. For the outer flow
\rho^{e}=1,U^{e}=(1,0)
, the behavior of the solution could be described by the Blasius solution, which is a self-similar solution of the steady Prandtl equation and takes the form

\displaystyle(u_{B},v_{B})=\Big(f^{\prime}(\zeta),\frac{1}{2\sqrt{x+x_{0}}}\{\zeta f^{\prime}(\zeta)-f(\zeta)\}\Big),

where
\zeta=\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu(x+x_{0})}}
with
x_{0}>0
as a free parameter, and the profile
f
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}ff^{\prime\prime}+f^{\prime\prime\prime}=0,\quad f(0)=f^{\prime}(0)=0,\quad\lim_{\zeta\to+\infty}f^{\prime}(\zeta)=1.

The Blasius solution has been experimentally confirmed with remarkable accuracy as a basic flow over a flat plate
[
25
]
.
In a remarkable work
[
21
]
, Iyer and Masmoudi justify the Prandtl expansion with the Blasius solution as a leading order approximation of the steady incompressible Navier-Stokes equations. In a recent work
[
18
]
, Guo, Wang and Zhang prove that the Blasius solution is also a stable solution of the unsteady Prandtl equation. This gives another indication why the Blasius solution is reasonable as a basic flow.
See
[
17
,
19
,
20
,
26
]
and references therein for more related works.

Considering a local region in the streamwise direction, the flow
U_{s}^{\nu}=\Big(U_{s}\big(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}}\big),0\Big)
with a Blasius profile
U_{s}(Y)
could be seen as an approximation of the Blasius solution. To predict the boundary layer transition, a key problem is to study the stability of the flow under the perturbations. To this end, we consider the linearized compressible Navier-Stokes system(CNS) around the boundary layer flow
(1,U_{s}^{\nu})
on
\Omega=[0,L]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
:

(1.2)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\varrho+U_{s}\partial_{x}\rho+\nabla\cdot U=0,\\
&\partial_{t}U+U_{s}\partial_{x}U+m^{-2}\nabla p+\partial_{y}U_{s}(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}})(U_{2},0)-\nu\Delta U-\lambda\nu\nabla(\nabla\cdot U)+\rho\partial_{Y}^{2}U_{s}=0,\\
&U|_{y=0}=\lim_{y\to+\infty}U(y)=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Here
m=\frac{1}{\sqrt{P^{\prime}(1)}}
is the Mach number and
\mu
is taken to be 1 for convenience.

Our goal is to construct the solution to the linearized CNS (
1.2
) with the following form

\displaystyle(\varrho,U_{1},U_{2})(t,x,y)=(\rho,u,v)(Y)e^{\mathrm{i}\alpha(X-c\tau)}+\mathrm{c}.\mathrm{c}.,

where

\displaystyle\tau=\frac{t}{\sqrt{\nu}},\quad X=\frac{x}{\sqrt{\nu}},\quad Y=\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}}.

Then
(\rho(Y),u(Y),v(Y))
satisfies the following steady system

(1.3)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathrm{i}\alpha(U_{s}-c)\rho+\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u,v)=0,\\
&\sqrt{\nu}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})u+\mathrm{i}\alpha\lambda\sqrt{\nu}\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u,v)-\mathrm{i}\alpha(U_{s}-c)u\\
&\qquad\quad-(\mathrm{i}\alpha m^{-2}+\sqrt{\nu}\partial_{Y}^{2}U_{s})\rho-v\partial_{Y}U_{s}=0,\\
&\sqrt{\nu}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})v+\lambda\sqrt{\nu}\partial_{Y}\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u,v)-\mathrm{i}\alpha(U_{s}-c)v-m^{-2}\partial_{Y}\rho=0,\end{aligned}\right.

where
\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u,v)=\mathrm{i}\alpha u+\partial_{Y}v
and
(u,v)
satisfies the non-slip boundary condition

(1.4)

\displaystyle u(0)=v(0)=\lim_{Y\to\infty}u(Y)=\lim_{Y\to\infty}v(Y)=0.

For convenience, we define the linear operator in (
1.3
) by
\mathcal{L}
.

In this paper, we will seek two kinds of important unstable modes, which are so called Tollmien-Schlichting (T-S) waves.

(1)

Temporal mode
:
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
c\in\mathbb{C}
. When
\alpha>0
and
\mathrm{Im}c=c_{i}>0
, this kind of solution will grow in time;

(2)

Spatial mode
:
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
and
\alpha c\in\mathbb{R}
. When
\mathrm{Im}\alpha=\alpha_{i}<0
, this kind of solution will grow along the streamwise direction
x
.
So, it is more relevant in physics and the growth rate
-\alpha_{i}
is a key quantity of
e^{N}
criterion in the boundary layer transition.

For the incompressible fluid, the construction of T-S waves started with a series of classical works by Heisenberg, Tollmien, Schlichting, C. C. Lin etc.
[
23
,
7
]
. Based on the asymptotic analysis, they found that there exist lower and upper marginal stability branches
\alpha_{low}(\nu),\alpha_{up}(\nu)
depending on the profile
U_{s}(y)
so that when
\alpha\in(\alpha_{low},\alpha_{up})
, the Orr-Sommerfeld equation exist unstable solutions with
\mathrm{Im}(c)>0
. In a breakthrough work
[
13
]
, Grenier, Guo and Nyugen constructed the T-S waves of temporal mode for
\alpha
lying in an interval [a,b] with
\alpha_{low}\ll a<b\ll\alpha_{up}
for generic flows
U_{s}(y)
. Moreover, they develop a robust Rayleigh-Airy iteration method to solve the Orr-Sommerfeld equation. Let us refer to
[
10
,
11
,
9
,
8
,
5
,
6
,
15
,
16
]
and references therein for more works on nonlinear stability(instability) of boundary layer flows. Let us refer to
[
24
,
14
,
1
,
3
,
4
]
for the linear stability results about the channel flow or pipe flow.

In a recent important work
[
27
]
, Yang and Zhang give a first rigorous construction of the compressible T-S waves of temporal mode for
\alpha
near the lower branch
\alpha_{low}
when the Mach number
m<\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
for a class of boundary layer profiles satisfying strong structure assumptions, which excludes the Blasius profile. Their proof does not use the Rayleigh-Airy iteration method developed in
[
13
]
. Instead, they developed a quai-compressible and Stokes iteration.

Throughout this paper, we make the following structure assumptions on the profile
U_{s}(Y)
:

(1.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&U_{s}(0)=0,\quad\lim_{Y\to\infty}U_{s}(Y)=1,\\
&0<U_{s}<1,\quad\text{ for }0<Y<\infty,\quad U_{s}^{\prime}(0)>0,\\
&\sup_{Y\geq 0}|e^{\eta_{0}Y}\partial_{Y}^{k}(U_{s}(Y)-1)|<+\infty,\quad k=0,1,\dots,4.\end{split}

Our main result is stated as follows.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
m<1
. Suppose that
U_{s}(Y)
satisfies the structure assumption (
1.5
). Then there exist
0<\nu(m)\ll 1
and
1\ll A_{0}<B_{0}
such that for any
\nu\leq\nu(m)
the following statement holds true. For any
\alpha\in\mathbb{G}=\big\{\alpha\in\mathbb{C}:\alpha_{r}=A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}\text{ with }A\in(A_{0},B_{0}),|\alpha_{i}|\leq\gamma\alpha_{r}\text{ with }\gamma\ll 1\big\}
, we can find
c(\alpha)
such that there exists a solution
(\varrho,U_{1},U_{2})
to the system (
1.2
) with the form of

\displaystyle(\varrho,U_{1},U_{2})(t,x,y)=(\rho,u,v)(\frac{y}{\sqrt{\nu}})e^{-\mathrm{i}c\alpha\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}t}e^{\mathrm{i}\alpha\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}x}+\mathrm{c}.\mathrm{c}.,

where
(\rho(Y),u(Y),v(Y))\in W^{1,\infty}\times W^{2,\infty}\times W^{2,\infty}
is a solution to the system (
1.3
) and (
1.4
). Moreover, such
c,\alpha
satisfy the following properties

\displaystyle c_{r}=U^{\prime}_{s}(0)^{-1}(1-m^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{r}+o(\alpha),\quad 0<c_{i}-U_{s}^{\prime}(0)^{-1}(1-m^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{i}\sim A^{-1}\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}.

In particular, for any
\alpha_{r}=A\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}
with
A\in(A_{0},B_{0})
, we have the following results:

•

there exists
\alpha^{0}=\alpha_{r}+\mathrm{i}\alpha^{0}_{i}\in\mathbb{G}
such that

(1.6)

\displaystyle\alpha^{0}c(\alpha^{0})\in\mathbb{R},\quad\alpha_{i}^{0}<0\text{ and }|\alpha_{i}^{0}|\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}.

•

there exists
c(\alpha_{r})
such that

(1.7)

\displaystyle c_{i}(\alpha_{r})>0\text{ and }|c_{i}(\alpha_{r})|\sim\nu^{\frac{1}{8}}.

Let us give some remarks on our results.

(1)

This is the first time that a mixed type unstable mode has been constructed, which will grow in both space and time:

\displaystyle\left|(\varrho,U_{1},U_{2})(t,x,y)\right|\sim e^{C\nu^{-\frac{1}{4}}t}e^{CA\nu^{-\frac{3}{8}}U^{\prime}_{s}(0)^{-1}(1-m^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{i}t}e^{-\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{i}x}.

The special case (
1.6
) is related to the T-S waves of spatial mode, which will grow in the space:

\displaystyle|(\varrho,U_{1},U_{2})(t,x,y)|\sim e^{C\nu^{-\frac{3}{8}}x}.

The special case (
1.7
) is related to the T-S waves of temporal mode, which will grow in the time:

\displaystyle|(\varrho,U_{1},U_{2})(t,x,y)|\sim e^{c\nu^{-\frac{1}{4}}t}

(2)

The Blasius profile satisfies the structure assumptions (
1.5
).

(3)

All the estimates in our paper are uniformly in
m\in(0,1)
. When
m\to 0
, we can construct the T-S waves of both temporal mode and spatial mode for the incompressible flow, where temporal mode was analyzed in
[
13
]
by Grenier-Guo-Nguyen. The vanishing Mach number limit for temporal mode was also obtained in the work of Yang and Zhang
[
27
]
.

(4)

Our method could be used to construct the T-S waves for
\alpha\in[a,b]
with
\alpha_{low}\ll a<b\ll\alpha_{up}
as in
[
13
]
.

(5)

For the supersonic flow
m>1
, there exist inviscid unstable modes, which plays more dominant role in the boundary layer transition
[
22
]
. This will be conducted in future works.

2.
Sketch of the proof

The key ingredient of the proof is to construct a non-trivial solution to the system (
1.3
).
To this end, we will introduce a new iteration scheme between the quasi-incompressible system (
2.1
)
and the quasi-compressible system(
2.4
) and start this iteration scheme with the slow or fast solution to the quasi-incompressible system (
2.1
). See the beginning of section 8 for the detailed description of iteration scheme.

First of all, motivated by
[
27
]
, we introduce the following quasi-incompressible system

(2.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathrm{i}\alpha(U_{s}-c)\rho_{os}+\mathrm{i}\alpha u_{os}+\partial_{Y}v_{os}=0,\\
&\sqrt{\nu}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})[u_{os}+(U_{s}-c)\rho_{os}]-\mathrm{i}\alpha(U_{s}-c)u_{os}-v_{os}\partial_{Y}U_{s}-\mathrm{i}\alpha m^{-2}\rho_{os}=f_{u},\\
&\sqrt{\nu}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})v_{os}-\mathrm{i}\alpha(U_{s}-c)v_{os}-m^{-2}\partial_{Y}\rho_{os}=f_{v}.\end{aligned}\right.

Compared with (
1.3
), the system (
2.1
) mainly drop all the divergence terms in the equations of velocity in (
1.3
) and add some terms such that we can derive an Orr-Sommerfeld type equation. Indeed, let us introduce the generalized stream function
\phi(Y)
defined by

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\phi(Y)=u_{os}+(U_{s}-c)\rho_{os},\quad-\mathrm{i}\alpha\phi(Y)=v_{os}.

Then the system (
2.1
) can be reduced to an Orr-Sommerfeld type system

(2.2)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\varepsilon\Lambda(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\phi-(U_{s}-c)\Lambda\phi+\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y}U_{s})\phi=\Omega(f_{u},f_{v}),\\
&\rho=m^{2}A^{-1}(Y)\left(\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\partial_{Y}\phi-(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}\phi+\partial_{Y}U_{s}\phi-\frac{f_{u}}{\mathrm{i}\alpha}\right),\end{aligned}\right.

where

(2.3)

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\frac{\sqrt{\nu}}{\mathrm{i}\alpha},\quad\Lambda=\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y})-\alpha^{2},\quad A(Y)=1-m^{2}(U_{s}-c)^{2},

and

\displaystyle\Omega(f_{u},f_{v})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathrm{i}\alpha}\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}f_{u})-f_{v}.

When
m=0
, the system (
2.2
) is reduced to the classical Orr-Sommerfeld equation. See section 6 for the details.

To control the compressible part, we introduce a quasi-compressible system as follows

(2.4)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathrm{i}\alpha(U_{s}-c)\rho_{st}+\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u_{st},v_{st})=0,\\
&\sqrt{\nu}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})u_{st}+\mathrm{i}\alpha\lambda\sqrt{\nu}\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u_{st},v_{st})\\
&\qquad\quad-\mathrm{i}\alpha(U_{s}-c)u_{st}-(\mathrm{i}\alpha m^{-2}+\sqrt{\nu}\partial_{Y}^{2}U_{s})\rho_{st}=q_{1},\\
&\sqrt{\nu}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})v_{st}+\lambda\sqrt{\nu}\partial_{Y}\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u_{st},v_{st})-\mathrm{i}\alpha(U_{s}-c)v_{st}\\
&\qquad\quad-\int_{Y}^{\infty}\mathrm{i}\alpha\partial_{Y}U_{s}v_{st}dY^{\prime}-m^{-2}\partial_{Y}\rho_{st}=q_{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

Compared with the system (
1.3
), the main difference in (
2.4
) is that we remove non-local term
-\partial_{Y}U_{s}v
in the second equation and add a new term
-\int_{Y}^{\infty}i\alpha\partial_{Y}U_{s}v_{st}dY^{\prime}
in the third equation. With theses modifications, we can derive a good equation for the density by taking divergence of the equations of velocity. Indeed, the density
\rho_{st}
satisfies

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}\alpha\sqrt{\nu}(1+\lambda)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})((U_{s}-c)\rho_{st})

\displaystyle-\alpha^{2}(U_{s}-c)^{2}\rho_{st}-m^{-2}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\rho_{st}

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}\alpha\sqrt{\nu}\partial_{Y}^{2}U_{s}\rho_{st}=\mathrm{i}\alpha q_{1}+\partial_{Y}q_{2}.

Furthermore, the density equation can be written as

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\beta^{2})\rho_{st}=&\alpha^{2}(A(Y)-A_{\infty})\rho_{st}-\mathrm{i}m^{2}\alpha\sqrt{\nu}(1+\lambda)(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})((U_{s}-c)\rho_{st})\\
&-\mathrm{i}\alpha m^{2}\sqrt{\nu}\partial_{Y}^{2}U_{s}\rho_{st}-\mathrm{i}\alpha m^{2}q_{1}-m^{2}\partial_{Y}q_{2},\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\beta=\alpha A_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad A_{\infty}=\lim_{Y\to+\infty}A(Y)=1-m^{2}(1-c)^{2}.

When
m<1
, this could be viewed as an elliptic equation. Once we obtain the density
\rho_{st}
, thus
\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u_{st},v_{st})
, then
u_{st}
can be recovered by solving the following Airy type equation

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})u_{st}-(U_{s}-c)u_{st}=(\mathrm{i}\alpha^{-1})q_{1}+\big(m^{-2}+\varepsilon\partial_{Y}^{2}U_{s}+\mathrm{i}\lambda\sqrt{\nu}\alpha(U_{s}-c)\big)\rho_{st}.

The vertical velocity
v_{st}
can be recovered by solving the following equation

\displaystyle(U_{s}-c)v_{st}+\int_{Y}^{\infty}\partial_{Y}(U_{s}-1)v_{st}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})v_{st}+\lambda\varepsilon\partial_{Y}\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u_{st},v_{st})-m^{-2}(\mathrm{i}\alpha)^{-1}\partial_{Y}\rho_{st}-(\mathrm{i}\alpha)^{-1}q_{2},

which can be written as

\displaystyle(1-c+\varepsilon\alpha^{2})v_{st}=

\displaystyle\int_{Y}^{\infty}(U_{s}-1)\partial_{Y}v_{st}dY^{\prime}+\varepsilon\partial_{Y}^{2}v_{st}+\lambda\varepsilon\partial_{Y}\mathrm{div}_{\alpha}(u_{st},v_{st})

\displaystyle-m^{-2}(\mathrm{i}\alpha)^{-1}\partial_{Y}\rho_{st}-(\mathrm{i}\alpha)^{-1}q_{2}.

See section 7 for the details.

Most of the paper will be devoted to solving the Orr-Sommerfeld type system:

(2.5)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Lambda(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\phi-(U_{s}-c)\Lambda\phi+\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y}U_{s})\phi=F.

In the incompressible case, Grenier, Guo and Nguyen
[
13
]
introduce a robust Rayleigh-Airy iteration scheme to solve the Orr-Sommerfeld equation. However, this iteration scheme does not work in the compressible case due to the fact that the commutator
[\Lambda,\partial_{Y}^{2}]
will lead to one loss of the derivative. Note that
[\Lambda,\partial_{Y}^{2}]=0
when
m=0
. To overcome this difficulty, we introduce a new Airy-Airy-Rayleigh
iteration scheme. See the beginning of section 5 for the details. To carry out this iteration scheme, we solve the Rayleigh type equation conducted in section 3:

\displaystyle(U_{s}-c)\Lambda\varphi-\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y}U_{s})\varphi=F.

The proof is based on an iteration argument in a well-chosen functional space.
In section 4, we solve the Airy type equation

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})\big(\partial_{Y}(A(Y)^{-1}\partial_{Y})-\alpha^{2}\big)\psi-(U_{s}-c)\big(\partial_{Y}(A(Y)^{-1}\partial_{Y})-\alpha^{2}\big)\psi=F.

To solve the Airy type equation, we introduce a modified Langer transform, which is the key to avoid using the analyticity of
U_{s}(Y)
. One of key ingredients is the estimate of the Green function related to the approximate Airy equation, see Lemma
4.4
.
Our approach is different from Yang and Zhang
[
27
]
. They made strong assumptions about the structure of boundary layer profiles, excluding the Blasius flow, to obtain energy estimates for the Airy-type equation (
2.5
) with Mach number
m<1/\sqrt{3}
. In contrast, our method provides pointwise estimates for
\phi
in the entire subsonic regime, and includes the Blasius flow by relaxing assumptions on the boundary layer profiles.

To construct the T-S waves, we construct the slow mode
(\rho^{s},u^{s},v^{s})
and fast mode
(\rho^{f},u^{f},v^{f})
to the system (
1.3
) with the boundary condition

\displaystyle\lim_{Y\to\infty}u^{s}(Y)=\lim_{Y\to\infty}v^{s}(Y)=\lim_{Y\to\infty}u^{f}(Y)=\lim_{Y\to\infty}v^{f}(Y)=0.

See section 8 for the construction, which is based on the iteration between the system (
2.1
)
and the system (
2.4
).

To match the non-slip boundary condition, we will find the solution
(\rho,u,v)
of the system (
1.3
) with the form

\displaystyle(\rho,u,v)=C_{s}(\rho^{s},u^{s},v^{s})+C_{f}(\rho^{f},u^{f},v^{f}),

where

\displaystyle C_{s}u^{s}(0)+C_{f}u^{f}(0)=0\text{ and }C_{s}v^{s}(0)+C_{f}v^{f}(0)=0.

The existence of
(C_{s},C_{f})
is guaranteed by the following dispersion relation:

\displaystyle\frac{u^{s}(0)}{v^{s}(0)}=\frac{u^{f}(0)}{v^{f}(0)}.

By our construction, we have an explicit asymptotic expansion for the main parts of the slow mode and fast mode.
In a suitable regime of
(\alpha,c)
, we can find a solution of the dispersion relation via an implicit function theorem.
See section 9 for the details.

Let us conclude this section by the following fact: for
m\in(0,1)
,
A(Y)
has upper and below bound, i.e., there exists
C>1
such that

(2.6)

\displaystyle 0<C^{-1}\leq A(Y)\leq C\quad\text{ for any }Y\geq 0.

3.
The Rayleigh type equation

As we mentioned in section 2, to solve the Orr-Sommerfeld type equation (
2.5
), we need to perform an Airy-Airy-Rayleigh iteration. In this section, we first solve the following Rayleigh type equation

(3.1)

\displaystyle(U_{s}-c)\Lambda\varphi-\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y}U_{s})\varphi=F,

where
\Lambda=\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y})-\alpha^{2}
with
A(Y)=1-m^{2}(U_{s}-c)^{2}
.
When
m=0
, this is reduced to the classical Rayleigh equation. For convenience, we denote

\displaystyle Ray[\cdot]=(U_{s}-c)\Lambda-\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y}U_{s}).

We construct the solution to the Rayleigh equation in the following functional spaces:

(3.2)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\theta}}=

\displaystyle|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\|(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{4}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{3}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{2}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{Y}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}},

and

(3.3)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}=\|(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{2}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|\partial_{Y}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}},

where

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{\theta}}=\|e^{\theta Y}f\|_{L^{\infty}}.

We denote

(3.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{1}=\big\{(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}:(|\alpha|+|c|)|\log c_{i}|\ll 1,\alpha_{r}\geq C_{0}|\alpha_{i}|,c_{i}>0\big\}.

In this section, we always assume that
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{1}
.

3.1.
Non-homogeneous Rayleigh equation

In this subsection, we construct the solution to (
3.1
) in the case when
e^{\theta Y}F\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, where
\theta>0
is a universe constant. Such results will be applied to construct a non-trivial solution to the homogeneous Rayleigh type equation (
3.11
) and perform the Airy-Airy-Rayleigh iteration in section 5.

According to the definition of
\Lambda
, the equation (
3.1
) can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\left(A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)^{2}\partial_{Y}\left(\frac{\varphi_{non}}{U_{s}-c}\right)\right)=F+\alpha^{2}(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{non}.

We use the method of iteration to construct a solution to (
3.1
). To this end, we define

(3.5)

\displaystyle\varphi^{(0)}_{non}(Y)=(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime},

which satisfies

(3.6)

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\left(A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)^{2}\partial_{Y}\left(\frac{\varphi^{(0)}_{non}}{U_{s}-c}\right)\right)=F.

For any
j\geq 0
, we define

(3.7)

\displaystyle\varphi_{non}^{(j+1)}(Y)=\alpha^{2}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y)\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}(U_{s}(Z)-c)\varphi_{non}^{(j)}(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime},

which satisfies

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{non}^{(j+1)}]=\alpha^{2}(U_{s}-c)(\varphi_{non}^{(j)}-\varphi_{non}^{(j+1)}).

Formally,
\varphi_{non}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\varphi_{non}^{(j)}
is a solution to (
3.1
).

Lemma 3.1
.

Suppose
|c|\ll 1
and
c_{i}\neq 0
. Let
Y_{c}
satisfy
U_{s}(Y_{c})-c_{r}=0
and
Y,Y_{0}
be constants such that
0\leq Y<Y_{c}<Y_{0}
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{G(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}-\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(U_{s}(Y)-c)}\Big|\leq C(\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}})|\log|c_{i}||.

Proof.

Thanks to
1=\frac{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\frac{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})-U_{s}^{\prime}(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}
, we get by integration by parts that

\displaystyle\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{G(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}G(Y^{\prime})d\Big(\frac{1}{U_{s}-c}\Big)+\frac{1}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{(U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})-U_{s}^{\prime})G(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}(Y)-c}-\frac{1}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\frac{G(Y_{0})}{U_{s}(Y_{0})-c}+\frac{1}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{\partial_{Y^{\prime}}G(Y^{\prime})}{U_{s}-c}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{(U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})-U_{s}^{\prime})G(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}(Y)-c}+I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

Due to
|U_{s}(Y_{0})-c|\geq C^{-1}>0
for
Y_{0}>Y_{c}
, we infer that

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq C|G(Y_{0})|\leq C\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Thanks to

\displaystyle\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{|U_{s}-c|}dY^{\prime}\leq C\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{|Y-Y_{c}|+|c_{i}|}dY^{\prime}\leq C|\log|c_{i}||,

we have

\displaystyle|I_{2}|\leq C\|\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{|U_{s}-c|}dY^{\prime}\leq C\|\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}|\log|c_{i}||,

\displaystyle|I_{3}|\leq C\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\Big|\frac{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})-U_{s}^{\prime}}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\Big|dY^{\prime}\leq C\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}|\log|c_{i}||,

by using the fact
\Big|\frac{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})-U_{s}^{\prime}}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\Big|\leq\frac{C}{|U_{s}-c|}
. Then the lemma follows.
∎

Proposition 3.2
.

If
e^{\theta Y}F\in L^{\infty}
, then there exists a solution
\varphi_{non}\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}
to (
3.1
) with

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{non}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

In addition, if
e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}^{j}F\in L^{\infty}
for
j=1,2
and
c_{i}\geq c_{0}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}>0
, then
\varphi_{non}\in W^{4,\infty}
satisfies

\displaystyle|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}|e^{\theta Y}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{3}\varphi_{non}(Y)|\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}+C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|e^{\theta Y}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{4}\varphi_{non}(Y)|\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}+C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}+C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\|e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}^{2}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

As a consequence, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{non}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\theta}}\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}+C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}\|e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}+C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}\|e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}^{2}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Proof.

By our assumption
0<|c|\ll 1
, there exists
Y_{0}>Y_{c}
such that for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|U_{s}(Y)-c|\geq C^{-1}>0.

Using (
2.6
) and (
3.5
), we infer that for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}\varphi_{non}^{(0)}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big|\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}e^{\theta(Y-Y^{\prime\prime})}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime}\Big|\leq C\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

For
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
, we decomposite
\varphi_{non}^{(0)}
into two parts:

\displaystyle\varphi_{non}^{(0)}(Y)=

\displaystyle(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}+(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}.

As above, for the first term, we have

(3.8)

\displaystyle\Big|e^{\theta Y}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}\Big|\leq C\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

For the second term, we get by Lemma
3.1
with
G(Y)=1
that

\displaystyle\Big|e^{\theta Y}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big|(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}},

which along with (
3.8
) shows that for
Y\geq 0
,

(3.9)

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}\varphi_{non}^{(0)}(Y)|\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Next we give the estimate of
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{non}^{(0)}
. By the definition of
\varphi_{non}^{(0)}
in (
3.5
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{non}^{(0)}(Y)=U^{\prime}_{s}(Y)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}-\frac{A(Y)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}(Y)-c)}.

Then for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}\varphi_{non}^{(0)}(Y)|\leq C\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

For
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
, notice that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{Y}\varphi_{non}^{(0)}(Y)=&U^{\prime}_{s}(Y)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}-\frac{A(Y)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}(Y)-c)}\\
&+U^{\prime}_{s}(Y)\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.\end{split}

For
I_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{3}|\leq

\displaystyle C\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Let
G(Y^{\prime})=A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}F(Z)dZ
. We write

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle U^{\prime}_{s}(Y)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{G(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U^{\prime}_{s}(Y)\Big(\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{G(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}-\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(U_{s}(Y)-c)}\Big)+\frac{U^{\prime}_{s}(Y)G(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(U_{s}(Y)-c)},

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle-\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}(Y)-c},

which yield that

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}=

\displaystyle U^{\prime}_{s}(Y)\Bigg(\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{G(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}-\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(U_{s}(Y)-c)}\Bigg)-\Bigg(1-\frac{U^{\prime}_{s}(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\Bigg)\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}(Y)-c}.

By (
2.6
), we have

\displaystyle\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}},\quad\|\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Then we apply Lemma
3.1
and the fact
\Big|(1-\frac{U^{\prime}_{s}(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})})\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}(Y)-c}\Big|\leq C\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}
to deduce that

\displaystyle|I_{1}+I_{2}|\leq C(\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{Y}G\|_{L^{\infty}})|\log c_{i}|\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}},

which along with the estimate of
I_{3}
shows that for
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{non}^{(0)}(Y)|\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

This proves that for any
Y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}\partial_{Y}\varphi_{non}^{(0)}(Y)|\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Then by using the equation (
3.6
), we infer that for any
Y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|e^{\theta Y}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{2}\varphi_{non}^{(0)}(Y)|\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Summing up, we conclude

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{non}^{(0)}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Using the formula (
3.7
) and the argument as above, we can show that for
j\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{non}^{(j+1)}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}\varphi_{non}^{(j)}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|\|\varphi_{non}^{(j)}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}\leq C(|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|)^{j+1}\|\varphi_{non}^{(0)}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}.

Since
(\alpha,c)\in\mathbb{H}_{1}
so that
|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{non}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\|\varphi_{non}^{(j)}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}\leq C\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|)^{j}\|\varphi_{non}^{(0)}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\varphi_{non}^{(0)}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}\leq C|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\theta Y}F\|_{L^{\infty}},

which gives the first statement.

In order to get the estimate for
\partial_{Y}^{k}\varphi_{non}
,
k=3,4
, we take
\partial_{Y}
and
\partial_{Y}^{2}
on (
3.1
) to have

\displaystyle A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{3}\varphi_{non}=

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}F-\partial_{Y}((U_{s}-c)A^{-1})\partial_{Y}^{2}\varphi_{non}

\displaystyle-\partial_{Y}\Big((U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}(A^{-1})\partial_{Y}\varphi_{non}-\alpha^{2}(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{non}-\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y}U_{s})\varphi_{non}\Big),

\displaystyle A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{4}\varphi_{non}=

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}^{2}F-2\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}(U_{s}-c))\partial_{Y}^{3}\varphi_{non}-\partial_{Y}^{2}((U_{s}-c)A^{-1})\partial_{Y}^{2}\varphi_{non}

\displaystyle-\partial_{Y}^{2}\Big((U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}(A^{-1})\partial_{Y}\varphi_{non}-\alpha^{2}(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{non}-\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y}U_{s})\varphi_{non}\Big).

Then we can conclude our result by using the estimate of
\|\varphi_{non}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\theta}}
, (
2.6
) and

(3.10)

\displaystyle|U_{s}-c|\geq c_{i}\geq c_{0}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}>0.

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

3.2.
Homogeneous Rayleigh equation

In this subsection, we construct a decay solution to the homogeneous Rayleigh equation:

(3.11)

\displaystyle(U_{s}-c)\Lambda\varphi_{Ray}-\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y}U_{s})\varphi_{Ray}=0.

Such solution
\varphi_{Ray}
is the main part of the slow mode of the homogeneous Orr-Sommerfeld equation (
2.5
).

We define

(3.12)

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)=2\beta e^{\beta Y}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Z)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}e^{2\beta Z}}dZ,

where

(3.13)

\displaystyle\beta=\alpha A_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}=\beta_{r}+i\beta_{i},\quad A_{\infty}=\lim_{Y\to+\infty}A(Y)=1-m^{2}(1-c)^{2}.

For
m\in(0,1),
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Re}A_{\infty}>0,\quad\mathrm{Im}A_{\infty}>0,

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{Re}A_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sim(1-m^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}>0,\quad\mathrm{Im}A_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sim m^{2}c_{i}>0,

which along with the fact
\alpha=\alpha_{r}+\mathrm{i}\alpha_{i}
with
\alpha_{r}\gg|\alpha_{i}|
implies

(3.14)

\displaystyle 0<C^{-1}|\alpha|\leq|\beta_{r}|\leq|\beta|\leq C|\alpha|.

By a direct calculation, we find that

(3.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}(U_{s}-c)\Lambda\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y}U_{s})\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}=&2\beta U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-1}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-\beta\frac{A^{\prime}}{A^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}\\
&+(A^{-1}\beta^{2}-\alpha^{2})(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}.\end{split}

We next define
\varphi_{R}
to be a solution to the following Rayleigh equation, which is constructed according to Proposition
3.2
:

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{R}]=

\displaystyle-2\beta U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-1}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}+\beta\frac{A^{\prime}}{A^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-(A^{-1}\beta^{2}-\alpha^{2})(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}.

Then
\varphi_{Ray}=\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}+\varphi_{R}
is a solution to (
3.11
).

We first give the asymptotic expansions of the main part
\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)
.

Lemma 3.3
.

There exists
Y_{0}>0
such that

•

For
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\Big|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y},

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\partial_{Y}((U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y})\Big|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

•

For
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}-\frac{2\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U^{\prime}_{s}(Y_{c})}\Big|\leq C|\alpha||U_{s}(Y)-c||\log c_{i}|,

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}U_{s}^{\prime}e^{-\beta Y}\Big|\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=-(1-m^{2})c+\frac{2\beta}{U^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big((|\alpha|+|c|)|c||\log c_{i}|\Big),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=U_{s}^{\prime}(0)(1-m^{2})+\mathcal{O}\Big((|\alpha|+|c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big).

•

If
c_{i}\geq c_{0}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}>0
, then we have

(3.16)

\displaystyle\big\|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\big\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

Proof.

Recall that there exists
Y_{0}>Y_{c}
such that for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|U_{s}(Y)-c|\geq C^{-1}>0.

By the definitions of
\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}
,
A(Y)
and
A_{\infty}
, we notice that for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\frac{A(Y)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{A(Y)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}+\frac{A_{\infty}}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{m^{2}((1-c)^{2}-(U_{s}-c)^{2})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}+A_{\infty}\frac{((1-c)^{2}-(U_{s}-c)^{2})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}(1-c)^{2}},

which implies that for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

(3.17)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{A(Y)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\Big|\leq Ce^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

Then we infer from (
3.14
) that

\displaystyle\left|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-2\beta(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-2\beta Z}dZ\right|

\displaystyle=\left|2\beta(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\Big(\frac{A(Z)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\Big)e^{-2\beta Z}dZ\right|

\displaystyle\leq C|\beta|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

A direct calculation gives

\displaystyle 2\beta(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-2\beta Z}dZ=(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}.

Thus, we infer that for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\Big|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

Now we turn to the estimates for the case of
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
. For this, we first decompose
\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}
as follows

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)=I_{1}(Y)+I_{2}(Y)+I_{3}(Y),

where

\displaystyle I_{1}(Y)=2\beta(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}e^{-2\beta Z}dZ,

\displaystyle I_{2}(Y)=2\beta(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{A(Z)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\right)e^{-2\beta Z}dZ,

\displaystyle I_{3}(Y)=2\beta(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{A(Z)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}e^{2\beta Z}}dZ.

By a similar argument as above, we can deduce that for any
Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle I_{1}(Y)=(U_{s}-c)\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}e^{\beta Y-2\beta Y_{0}}=(U_{s}-c)\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}e^{-\beta Y}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||U_{s}-c|),

\displaystyle|I_{2}(Y)|\leq C|\beta||U_{s}-c|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}\leq C|\alpha||U_{s}-c|.

We write
I_{3}(Y)
as follows

\displaystyle I_{3}(Y)=2\beta(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}e^{2\beta Z}}dZ-2\beta m^{2}(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\beta Z}dZ.

For the second term of
I_{3}(Y)
, we notice that for any
Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|2\beta m^{2}(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}e^{-2\beta Z}dZ\right|\leq C|\alpha(U_{s}-c)|.

For the first term in
I_{3}(Y)
, we get by Lemma
3.1
with
G(Y)=e^{-2\beta Y}
and
\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C
,
\|G^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C|\alpha|
that

\displaystyle 2\beta(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}e^{2\beta Z}}dZ=

\displaystyle 2\beta(U_{s}-c)e^{\beta Y}\Big(\frac{e^{-2\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(U_{s}-c)}+\mathcal{O}(|\log c_{i}|)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha(U_{s}-c)||\log c_{i}|).

This shows that for any
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}-\frac{2\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U^{\prime}_{s}(Y_{c})}\Big|\leq C|\alpha||U_{s}(Y)-c||\log c_{i}|.

In particular, by the fact
A_{\infty}=1-m^{2}+\mathcal{O}(|c|)
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=-(1-m^{2})c+\frac{2\beta}{U^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big((|\alpha|+|c|)|c||\log c_{i}|\Big).

Next we give the estimate of
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}.

For
Y\geq Y_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\partial_{Y}\left((U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{Y}\Big(2\beta e^{\beta Y}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\Big[\frac{A(Z)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\Big]e^{-2\beta Z}\Big)dZ,

which along with (
3.17
) implies that

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\partial_{Y}\Big((U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\Big)\right|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

For
Y\leq Y_{0}
, we write

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}=

\displaystyle\beta\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-2\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}-c}A(Y)+2\beta e^{\beta Y}U_{s}^{\prime}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Z)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}e^{2\beta Z}}dZ

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-2\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}-c}A(Y)+II.

Using the fact that

\displaystyle\Big|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}-\frac{2\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U^{\prime}_{s}(Y_{c})}\Big|\leq C|\alpha||U_{s}(Y)-c||\log c_{i}|,

we have

(3.18)

\displaystyle|\beta\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha||U_{s}-c|+C|\alpha|^{2}+C|\alpha||U_{s}-c||\log c_{i}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha||U_{s}-c||\log c_{i}|+C|\alpha|^{2}.

We decompose
II
into the following parts:

\displaystyle II=

\displaystyle 2\beta e^{\beta Y}U_{s}^{\prime}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}e^{-2\beta Z}dZ

\displaystyle+2\beta e^{\beta Y}U_{s}^{\prime}\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\Bigg(\frac{A(Z)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\Bigg)e^{-2\beta Z}dZ

\displaystyle-2\beta e^{\beta Y}U_{s}^{\prime}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}e^{-2\beta Z}dZ-2\beta e^{\beta Y}U_{s}^{\prime}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}m^{2}e^{-2\beta Z}dZ

\displaystyle+2\beta e^{\beta Y}U_{s}^{\prime}\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}e^{-2\beta Z}dZ

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U_{s}^{\prime}e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}+II_{2}+II_{3}+II_{4}+II_{5}.

By (
3.17
), we have

\displaystyle|II_{2}|\leq C|\alpha|.

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle|II_{3}|+|II_{4}|\leq C|\alpha|.

So, we focus on
II_{5}.
By Lemma
3.1
with
G(Y)=e^{-2\beta Y}
, we have

\displaystyle II_{5}=

\displaystyle 2\beta e^{\beta Y}U_{s}^{\prime}\Big(\frac{e^{-2\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(U_{s}-c)}+\mathcal{O}(|\log c_{i}|)\Big)=\frac{2\beta e^{-\beta Y}U_{s}^{\prime}(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(U_{s}-c)}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|).

Then we have

\displaystyle II_{5}-2\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}-c}A(Y)=

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta e^{-\beta Y}U_{s}^{\prime}(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(U_{s}-c)}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|)-2\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}-c}A(Y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}-c}\Big(\frac{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}-1\Big)+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(|\alpha|)+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|)=\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|).

Thus, we arrive at

\displaystyle\Big|II-U_{s}^{\prime}e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}-\frac{2\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}-c}A(Y)\Big|\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|,

which along with (
3.18
) shows that for
Y\leq Y_{0}

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-U_{s}^{\prime}e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\Big|\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(0)=U_{s}^{\prime}(0)(1-m^{2})+\mathcal{O}\Big((|\alpha|+|c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big).

It remains to prove (
3.16
). We denote

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}=\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)},\quad\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)}=\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}.

A direct calculation gives

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)}]=-2\beta U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-2}\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)}+(A^{-1}\beta^{2}-\alpha^{2})(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)},

which along with (
3.15
) gives

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}]=

\displaystyle 2\beta U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-1}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-\beta\frac{A^{\prime}}{A^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}+2\beta U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-2}\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)}

\displaystyle+(A^{-1}\beta^{2}-\alpha^{2})(U_{s}-c)(\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)}):=F_{\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}}.

According to the above results, we know that

\displaystyle|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}|,~|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}|\leq C,\quad|\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)}|,~|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)}|,~|\partial_{Y}^{2}\varphi_{Ray,0}^{(0)}|\leq C,

which along with the facts that
|U_{s}^{\prime}|,~|A^{\prime}|\leq Ce^{-\eta_{0}Y}
and
|A^{-1}\beta^{2}-\alpha^{2}|\leq C|\alpha|^{2}e^{-\eta_{0}Y}
, show that

\displaystyle\|e^{\eta_{0}Y}F_{\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C|\alpha|,\quad\|e^{\eta_{0}Y}\partial_{Y}F_{\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C|\alpha|.

Then we get by Proposition
3.2
that

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

Especially, we have

\displaystyle\|e^{\eta_{0}Y}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{2}\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|,

which implies

\displaystyle\|e^{\eta_{0}Y}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{2}F_{\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

If
c_{i}\geq c_{0}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}>0
, we apply Proposition
3.2
again to conclude that

\displaystyle|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}|e^{\eta_{0}Y}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{3}\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|,

\displaystyle|\varepsilon|^{\frac{2}{3}}|e^{\eta_{0}Y}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{4}\varphi_{Ray,R}^{(0)}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|,

which give (
3.16
).
∎

Recall that

(3.19)

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}]=

\displaystyle 2\beta U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-1}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-\beta\frac{A^{\prime}}{A^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}+(A^{-1}\beta^{2}-\alpha^{2})(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}.

Since
\varphi_{R}
is constructed in Proposition
3.2
, we can decompose it into
\varphi_{R}(Y)=\varphi_{1}(Y)+\varphi_{2}(Y)+\varphi_{3}(Y)+\varphi_{4}(Y)
, where

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\alpha^{2}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}(A^{-1}A_{\infty}-1)(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime},

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(Y)=

\displaystyle-2\beta(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-1}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime},

\displaystyle\varphi_{3}(Y)=

\displaystyle\beta(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}\frac{A^{\prime}}{A^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime},

and
\varphi_{4}(Y)
is the solution to

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{4}]=\alpha^{2}(U_{s}-c)(\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2}+\varphi_{3}).

Lemma 3.4
.

It holds that

•

For
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\varphi_{R}(Y)|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y},\quad|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(Y)|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

•

For
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{R}(Y)=-\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|U_{s}-c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(Y)=\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|).

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{R}(0)=-\frac{\beta}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big((|\alpha|^{2}+|c|^{2})|\log c_{i}|\Big),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(0)=\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|).

•

If
c_{i}\geq c_{0}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}>0
, then we have

(3.20)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{R}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

Proof.

By Proposition 3.2 and Lemma 3.3, we have

(3.21)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{4}\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|\|\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2}+\varphi_{3}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}.

Hence, to obtain the estimates of
\varphi_{R}
and
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}
, it enough to show the control of
\varphi_{i}
and
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
.

We first estimate
\varphi_{1}(Y)
. Using the fact that

(3.22)

\displaystyle\frac{A_{\infty}}{A}-1=\frac{A_{\infty}-A}{A}=m^{2}(U_{s}-1)(U_{s}+1-2c),

we get by (
1.5
) and (
2.6
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{A_{\infty}}{A}-1\Big|\leq Ce^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

From the estimate of
\varphi_{non}^{(0)}
in the proof of Proposition
3.2
and Lemma
3.3
, we deduce that for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

(3.23)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha|^{2}e^{-\eta_{0}Y}\|e^{\eta_{0}Y}(A^{-1}A_{\infty}-1)(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C|\alpha|^{2}e^{-\eta_{0}Y},

and for
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

(3.24)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|\|e^{\eta_{0}Y}(A^{-1}A_{\infty}-1)(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|.

We next estimate
\varphi_{2}(Y)
and
\varphi_{3}(Y)
. For
Y\geq Y_{0}
, similar to the estimate of
\varphi_{1}(Y)
along with the fact

\displaystyle|A^{\prime}|\leq CU_{s}^{\prime}\leq Ce^{-\eta_{0}Y},

we infer that

\displaystyle|\varphi_{2}(Y)|+|\varphi_{3}(Y)|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

This along with (
3.23
) implies that for
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

(3.25)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{1}(Y)+\varphi_{2}(Y)+\varphi_{3}(Y)|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

For
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
, we divide
\varphi_{2}(Y)
and
\varphi_{3}(Y)
as follows

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(Y)=

\displaystyle-2\beta\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-1}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s}-c)dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle-2\beta(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-1}W_{1}(Y^{\prime\prime})dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}(Y)+\varphi_{2,2}(Y),

and

\displaystyle\varphi_{3}(Y)=

\displaystyle\beta\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}\frac{A^{\prime}}{A^{2}}(U_{s}-c)^{2}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\beta(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}\frac{A^{\prime}}{A^{2}}(U_{s}-c)W_{1}(Y^{\prime\prime})dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{3,1}(Y)+\varphi_{3,2}(Y),

where

\displaystyle W_{1}(Y)=\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}(Y)-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}.

By Lemma
3.3
, we have

(3.26)

\displaystyle|W_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y},\quad Y\geq Y_{0},

(3.27)

\displaystyle|W_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|,\quad 0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}.

As in the estimate of
\varphi_{1}
, by using (
3.26
)-(
3.27
), (
2.6
) and the fact

\displaystyle|A^{\prime}|\leq CU_{s}^{\prime}\leq Ce^{-\eta_{0}Y},

we infer that

(3.28)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{2,2}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|,

(3.29)

\displaystyle|\varphi_{3,2}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|.

We get by integration by parts that

\displaystyle 2\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-1}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s}-c)dY^{\prime\prime}=

\displaystyle\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}A^{-1}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}\partial_{Y^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s}-c)^{2}dY^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}A^{-1}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s}-c)^{2}dY^{\prime\prime}+\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}\frac{A^{\prime}}{A^{2}}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s}-c)^{2}dY^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle-A^{-1}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime}}(U_{s}(Y^{\prime})-c)^{2}.

As a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}(Y)+\varphi_{3,1}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\beta^{2}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}A^{-1}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}(U_{s}-c)^{2}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\beta\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}=\varphi_{2,1}^{1}(Y)+\varphi_{2,1}^{2}(Y).

It is easy to see that

(3.30)

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}^{2}(Y)=

\displaystyle(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}.

For
\varphi_{2,1}^{1}(Y)
, we decompose it as follows

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}^{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\beta^{2}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)^{2}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\beta^{2}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)^{2}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}^{11}(Y)+\varphi_{2,1}^{12}(Y).

Using the fact (
2.6
) and a similar process for (
3.17
) that for
Y^{\prime\prime}\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\Big|A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)^{2}-\frac{(1-c)^{2}}{A_{\infty}}\Big|\leq Ce^{-\eta_{0}Y^{\prime\prime}},

we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}\Big(A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)^{2}-\frac{(1-c)^{2}}{A_{\infty}}\Big)e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}dY^{\prime\prime}\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}Y^{\prime\prime}}dY^{\prime\prime}\leq Ce^{-\eta_{0}Y^{\prime}},

and

\displaystyle\Big|\beta^{2}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}\Big(A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)^{2}-\frac{(1-c)^{2}}{A_{\infty}}\Big)e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}dY^{\prime\prime}dY^{\prime}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C|\alpha|^{2}|U_{s}-c|\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}e^{-\eta_{0}Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}\leq C|\alpha|^{2}.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\int_{Y}^{\infty}\frac{(1-c)^{2}}{A_{\infty}}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}=\beta^{-1}\frac{(1-c)^{2}}{A_{\infty}}e^{-\beta Y}.

Thus, we conclude that for
Y\leq Y_{0}
,

(3.31)

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}^{11}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\beta(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})e^{-\beta Y^{\prime}}}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha|^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\beta\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\beta(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y_{0}}^{+\infty}\Big(\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}\Big)e^{-\beta Y^{\prime}}dY^{\prime}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha|^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|U_{s}-c|)\Big),

where we used (
3.17
) in the last step.

Noticing that

\displaystyle\beta\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}A^{-1}(U_{s}-c)^{2}e^{-\beta Y^{\prime\prime}}dY^{\prime\prime}=\frac{e^{-\beta Y^{\prime}}(1-c)^{2}}{A_{\infty}}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha|)=\frac{(1-c)^{2}}{A_{\infty}}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha|),

we infer that

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}^{12}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\beta\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{A(Y)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\Big(\frac{(1-c)^{2}}{A_{\infty}}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha|)\Big)dY

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\beta(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\beta(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|U_{s}-c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big),

where we used Lemma
3.1
with
G(Y)=A(Y)
so that

\displaystyle\Big|(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{A(Y)}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY\Big|\leq C|\log c_{i}|

in the second step and
A(Y)=1-m^{2}(U_{s}-c)^{2}
in the last step.
By Lemma
3.1
again, we have

\displaystyle\beta(U_{s}-c)\int_{Y}^{Y_{0}}\frac{1}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY=\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||U_{s}-c||\log c_{i}|).

Thus, we obtain that for
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

(3.32)

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}^{12}(Y)=-\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|U_{s}-c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big).

Summing up (
3.31
) and (
3.32
), we conclude that for
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
,

(3.33)

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}^{1}(Y)=-(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}-\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|U_{s}-c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big).

Then we infer from (
3.30
) and (
3.33
) that

\displaystyle\varphi_{2,1}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|U_{s}-c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big),

which along with (
3.28
)-(
3.29
) gives

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(Y)+\varphi_{3}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|U_{s}-c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big).

This along with (
3.25
) shows that for
Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(Y)+\varphi_{2}(Y)+\varphi_{3}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|U_{s}-c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big),

which along with (
3.21
) and (
3.25
) implies

(3.34)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{4}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}+\|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{4}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|\|\varphi_{1}+\varphi_{2}+\varphi_{3}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha|^{3}|\log c_{i}|.

Therefore, by the definition of
\varphi_{R}
and the above estimates, we infer that for
Y\leq Y_{0}

\displaystyle\varphi_{R}(Y)=-\frac{\beta e^{-\beta Y}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}\Big(|\alpha|(|\alpha|+|U_{s}-c|)|\log c_{i}|\Big).

In the following, we compute
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(Y)
. We first give the estimate of
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}(Y)
. A direct calculation gives

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\alpha^{2}U_{s}^{\prime}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{A(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{\infty}(A^{-1}A_{\infty}-1)(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}dZdY^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\alpha^{2}\frac{A(Y)}{U_{s}-c}\int_{Y}^{\infty}(A^{-1}A_{\infty}-1)(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}dY^{\prime}.

As in the estimate of
\varphi_{1}
and using the fact
|A^{-1}A_{\infty}-1|\leq C|U_{s}-1|\leq Ce^{-\eta_{0}Y}
, it is easy to see that for
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha|^{2}e^{-\eta_{0}Y}.

For
0\leq Y\leq Y_{0}
, let
G(Y)=A(Y)\int_{Y}^{\infty}(A^{-1}A_{\infty}-1)(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}dY^{\prime}
, which holds that

\displaystyle|G(Y)|\leq C,\quad|G^{\prime}(Y)|\leq C.

Then we get by Lemma
3.1
that

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\alpha^{2}U_{s}^{\prime}\int_{Y}^{+\infty}\frac{G(Y^{\prime})}{(U_{s}-c)^{2}}dY^{\prime}+\alpha^{2}\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}-c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\alpha^{2}U_{s}^{\prime}\Big(\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(U_{s}-c)}+\mathcal{O}(|\log c_{i}|)\Big)+\alpha^{2}\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}-c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\frac{G(Y)}{U_{s}-c}\Big(1-\frac{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(|\alpha|^{2}|\log c_{i}|),

where we used
\Big|(U_{s}-c)^{-1}\Big(1-\frac{U_{s}^{\prime}(0)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\Big)\Big|\leq C
in the last step.

Next we give the estimates of
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{2}(Y),\partial_{Y}\varphi_{3}(Y).

Thanks to
|A^{\prime}|\leq C|U_{s}^{\prime}|\leq Ce^{-\eta_{0}Y}
and (
2.6
), we use the same argument in
\partial_{Y}\varphi_{1}(Y)
to infer that for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{2}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y},

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{3}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y},

and for
Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{2}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{3}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

Summing up the above estimates and applying (
3.34
) , we conclude that for any
Y\geq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(Y)|\leq C|\alpha|e^{-\eta_{0}Y},

and for
Y\leq Y_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\varphi_{R}(Y)|\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

It remains to prove (
3.20
).
Recall the equation of
\varphi_{R}
in (
3.19
):

\displaystyle Ray[\varphi_{R}]=F_{\varphi_{R}},

where

\displaystyle F_{\varphi_{R}}=

\displaystyle-2\beta U_{s}^{\prime}A^{-1}\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}+\beta\frac{A^{\prime}}{A^{2}}(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}-(A^{-1}\beta^{2}-\alpha^{2})(U_{s}-c)\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}.

By Lemma
3.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{Ray}^{(0)}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C,

which implies

\displaystyle\|e^{\eta_{0}Y}F_{\varphi_{R}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|e^{\eta_{0}Y}\partial_{Y}F_{\varphi_{R}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|e^{\eta_{0}Y}(U_{s}-c)\partial_{Y}^{2}F_{\varphi_{R}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq

\displaystyle C|\alpha|.

Then it follows from Proposition
3.2
that

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{R}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

∎

With Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
3.4
in hand, we directly conclude that

Proposition 3.5
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
3.4
, there exists a solution
\varphi_{Ray}\in\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{\beta_{r}}
to (
3.11
), which holds that

\displaystyle\varphi_{Ray}(0)=-(1-m^{2})c+\frac{\beta}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}+\mathcal{O}((|\alpha|^{2}+|c|^{2})|\log c_{i}|),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}(0)=(1-m^{2})U_{s}^{\prime}(0)+\mathcal{O}(|\alpha||\log c_{i}|).

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{Ray}(0)}{\partial_{Y}\varphi_{Ray}(0)}=-\frac{c}{U_{s}^{\prime}(0)}+\frac{\beta}{(1-m^{2})(U_{s}^{\prime}(0))^{2}}+\mathcal{O}((|\alpha|^{2}+|c|^{2})|\log c_{i}|).

In addition, if
c_{i}\geq C_{0}|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{Ray}-\frac{A_{\infty}}{(1-c)^{2}}(U_{s}-c)e^{-\beta Y}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\eta_{0}}}\leq C|\alpha||\log c_{i}|.

4.
The Airy type equation

This section is devoted to constructing the solution to the Airy type equation:

(4.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})w-(U_{s}-c)w=F,\quad Y>0,\\
&(\partial_{Y}(A(Y)^{-1}\partial_{Y})-\alpha^{2})\psi=w,\quad Y>0,\\
&\lim_{Y\to\infty}\psi(Y)=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Here
\varepsilon=\frac{\sqrt{\nu}}{\mathrm{i}\alpha}
,
c=c_{r}+\mathrm{i}c_{i}\in\mathbb{C}
,
\alpha=\alpha_{r}+\mathrm{i}\alpha_{i}=|\alpha|e^{-3\theta_{0}\mathrm{i}}\in\mathbb{C}
with
|\alpha_{i}|\ll|\alpha_{r}|
and
\theta_{0}\in(-\frac{\pi}{100},\frac{\pi}{100})
.

The related results in this section will be applied to perform the Airy-Airy-Rayleigh iteration and the construction of fast mode of the homogeneous Orr-Sommerfeld equation (
2.5
) in section 5. Moreover, they are also used to construct the tangential velocity
u_{st}
of the quasi-compressible system (
7.1
) in section 7.

4.1.
Langer transform and Airy function

Since we construct a solution to (
4.1
) for
Y\geq 0
, we introduce the following modified Langer transform instead of linear approximation
Y-Y_{c}
. In details, we define

\displaystyle\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(\frac{3}{2}\int_{Y_{c}}^{Y}\left(\frac{U_{s}(Z)-c_{r}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dZ\right)^{\frac{2}{3}},\quad Y\geq Y_{c}\\
-\left(\frac{3}{2}\int^{Y_{c}}_{Y}\left(\frac{c_{r}-U_{s}(Z)}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dZ\right)^{\frac{2}{3}},\quad Y\leq Y_{c},\end{array}\right.

and
\eta_{in}^{La}(Y)=Y-Y_{c}
. It is easy to verify that

(4.4)

\displaystyle U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})\eta_{out}^{La}(\partial_{Y}\eta_{out}^{La})^{2}=U_{s}-c_{r}.

Now we introduce the modified Langer transformation

(4.5)

\displaystyle\eta^{La}(Y)=\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)+\mathrm{i}\eta_{i}^{La},

where
\eta_{i}^{La}=-U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-1}c_{i}
and

(4.6)

\displaystyle\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)=\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\eta_{in}^{La}(Y)+\Big(1-\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\Big)\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)

with
\chi(\cdot)
a smooth function in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying
\chi(Y)\equiv 1
on
[0,1]
and
\chi(Y)=0
on
[2,+\infty)
, and
\kappa=|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{\frac{1}{3}}
.

We introduce the modified Airy function

\displaystyle A_{1}(Y)=-\mathrm{i}e^{-2\theta_{0}\mathrm{i}}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{1}{3}}Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)),

\displaystyle A_{2}(Y)=2\pi Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{5\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)).

We define

\displaystyle A_{1}(1,Y)=-\int_{Y}^{+\infty}A_{1}(Z)dZ,\qquad A_{1}(2,Y)=-\int_{Y}^{+\infty}A_{1}(1,Z)dZ,

\displaystyle A_{2}(1,Y)=\int_{0}^{Y}A_{2}(Z)dZ,\qquad A_{2}(2,Y)=\int_{0}^{Y}A_{2}(1,Z)dZ.

By the properties of Airy function and the above definitions, we can derive that for
j=1,2
,

(4.7)

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})A_{j}(Y)-(U_{s}-c)A_{j}(Y)=Err_{1}(Y)A_{j}+Err_{2}(Y)\partial_{Y}A_{j},

where

\displaystyle Err_{1}(Y)=U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})\eta^{La}(\partial_{Y}\eta^{La})^{2}-\varepsilon\alpha^{2}-(U_{s}-c),\quad Err_{2}(Y)=\frac{\varepsilon\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta^{La}(Y)}{\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)}.

We shall see that
Err_{1}
and
Err_{2}
are small errors actually.
Moreover, by the property of Airy function, we find that for any
z\in\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle Ai(z)(Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{3}}z))^{\prime}-Ai^{\prime}(z)Ai(e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{3}}z)=\frac{1}{2\pi}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{\pi}{6}},

which implies

(4.8)

\displaystyle A_{1}(Y)\partial_{Y}A_{2}(Y)-\partial_{Y}A_{1}(Y)A_{2}(Y)=\varepsilon^{-1}\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y).

For given source term
e^{\vartheta Y}F\in L^{\infty}
, we define

(4.9)

\displaystyle w_{app}(Y)=

\displaystyle A_{1}(Y)\int_{0}^{Y}A_{2}(Z)\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Z)^{-1}F(Z)dZ

\displaystyle+A_{2}(Y)\int_{Y}^{+\infty}A_{1}(Z)\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Z)^{-1}F(Z)dZ,

and

(4.10)

\displaystyle\psi_{app}(Y)=\int_{Y}^{+\infty}A(Y^{\prime})\int_{Y^{\prime}}^{+\infty}w_{app}(Z)dZ.

Then we find that
w_{app}
is a solution to the following equations

(4.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon(\partial_{Y}^{2}-\alpha^{2})w_{app}-(U_{s}-c)w_{app}=F+Err_{1}w_{app}+Err_{2}\partial_{Y}w_{app},\\
&\partial_{Y}(A^{-1}\partial_{Y})\psi_{app}(Y)=w_{app}(Y),\quad\quad\lim_{Y\to\infty}\psi_{app}(Y)=\lim_{Y\to\infty}w_{app}(Y)=0,\end{split}

where
A(Y)=1-m^{2}(U_{s}-c)^{2}
. We will show that (
4.11
) is an appropriate approximation of (
4.1
).

In what follows, we decompose
\mathbb{R}_{+}\cup\{0\}=\mathcal{N}^{-}\cup\mathcal{N}\cup\mathcal{N}^{+},
where

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{+}=\{Y\geq Y_{c}:|\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)|\geq 3M\},\quad\mathcal{N}=\{Y:|\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)|\leq 3M\},

(4.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{-}=\{0\leq Y\leq Y_{c}:|\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)|\geq 3M\}.

4.2.
Estimates of the Airy function

Since we introduce the modified Langer transformation involved in the construction of
w_{app}
, we need to obtain some useful estimates about
\eta^{La}(Y)
and the modified Airy function.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
|\alpha|,|\varepsilon|,|c|\ll 1
and
c_{r}>0
. Let
0<L\leq 1
be a small number in Lemma
A.6
. Then
\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
satisfies that following properties:

(1)

For any
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L
,

\displaystyle|\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)-(Y-Y_{c})|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|^{2},

and for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L
,

\displaystyle C^{-1}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{\frac{2}{3}}\leq|\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{\frac{2}{3}}.

(2)

There exits
0<C_{1}\leq C_{2}<+\infty
such that for any
Y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle C_{1}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{1}{3}}\leq|\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|\leq C_{2}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

Moreover, for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)-1|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|.

(3)

For any
Y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{4}{3}}.

Remark 4.2
.

From the above Lemma, we know that the real part of the modified Langer transformation
\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)
behaves like
Y-Y_{c}
if
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L
. Such region is quite larger than the thickness of sublayer
|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}
. It means that the Langer transformation is nearly linear in the sublayer. The imaginary part of
\eta^{La}(Y)
is a constant, which implies that
\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)\sim 1
in the sublayer.

Proof.

We first show the results about the bounds of
\eta^{La}_{r}(Y)
. According to Lemma
A.6
, we know that for any
0<|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L
(
c_{r}\ll L\leq 1
is a small constant in Lemma
A.6
),

(4.14)

\displaystyle|\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)-(Y-Y_{c})|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|^{2}.

By the definition (
4.5
), we know that for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq\kappa^{-1}M
,

\displaystyle\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)=Y-Y_{c},

and for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\in[\kappa^{-1}M,2\kappa^{-1}M]
,

\displaystyle\eta_{r}^{La}=

\displaystyle\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)(Y-Y_{c})+\Big(1-\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\Big)\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(Y-Y_{c})+\Big(1-\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\Big)(\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)-(Y-Y_{c})),

and for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq 2\kappa^{-1}M
,

\displaystyle\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)=\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)=(Y-Y_{c})+(\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)-(Y-Y_{c})).

Since
\kappa^{-1}M\ll 1
, we obtain that for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L
,

\displaystyle|\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)-(Y-Y_{c})|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|^{2}.

Again by Lemma
A.6
, we know that for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L
,

\displaystyle C^{-1}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{\frac{2}{3}}\leq|\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{\frac{2}{3}},

from which and the definition (
4.6
), we infer that for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L
,

\displaystyle C^{-1}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{\frac{2}{3}}\leq|\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{\frac{2}{3}}.

This proves the first statement of the lemma.

Now we turn to show the results for
\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}^{La}
. By the definition of
\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)
, we know that

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}_{r}(Y)=

\displaystyle\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)+\Big(1-\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\Big)\partial_{Y}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)

\displaystyle+(\kappa^{-1}M)^{-1}\chi^{\prime}\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)(Y-Y_{c}-\eta_{out}^{La}(Y))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\Big(1-\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\Big)(\partial_{Y}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)-1)

\displaystyle+(\kappa^{-1}M)^{-1}\chi^{\prime}\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)(Y-Y_{c}-\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)),

which gives

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)-1|\leq C|\partial_{Y}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)-1|+C(\kappa^{-1}M)^{-1}\Big|\chi^{\prime}\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\Big||\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)-(Y-Y_{c})|.

By Lemma
A.6
, we have

(4.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\partial_{Y}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)-1|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|,\qquad|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L,\end{split}

which along with (
4.14
) show that for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq 2\kappa^{-1}M
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)-1|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|.

For any
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L
, we know that

\displaystyle C_{2}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{2}{3}}\geq(\partial_{Y}\eta_{out}^{La})^{2}(Y)=\frac{U_{s}(Y)-c_{r}}{U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)}\geq C_{1}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{2}{3}}.

And for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq 2\kappa^{-1}M
,
\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)=\partial_{Y}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)
. Thus, we deduce that

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)-1|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|,\quad|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L,

\displaystyle C_{1}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{1}{3}}\leq|\partial_{Y}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|\leq C_{2}(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{1}{3}},\quad|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L.

This finishes the proof second statement of the lemma.

Next we show the results for
\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{r}^{La}
. We notice that

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)=

\displaystyle(\kappa^{-1}M)^{-2}\chi^{\prime\prime}\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\big((Y-Y_{c})-\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)\big)

\displaystyle+2(\kappa^{-1}M)^{-1}\chi^{\prime}\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)(1-\partial_{Y}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y))

\displaystyle+\Big(1-\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\Big)\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y).

By Lemma
A.6
, we know that for any
|Y-Y_{c}|>0
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{4}{3}}.

Therefore, for
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq 2\kappa^{-1}M
, we get by (
4.14
) and (
4.15
) that

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|Y-Y_{c}|^{2}+C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|Y-Y_{c}|+C\leq C.

For
|Y-Y_{c}|>2\kappa^{-1}M
, we know that

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|=|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{4}{3}}.

Therefore, we obtain that for any
Y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{4}{3}}.

∎

Based on the above lemma, we show the following estimates about the errors
Err_{1}(Y)
and
Err_{2}(Y)
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
|\alpha|,|\varepsilon|,|c|\ll 1
and
c_{r}>0
,
|c_{i}|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{1}{3}}
. Let
0<L\leq 1
be a small number in Lemma
A.6
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|(|c_{i}|+|\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|)+C|\varepsilon||\alpha|^{2},\qquad|Y-Y_{c}|\leq 2\kappa^{-1}M

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|c_{i}||\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|+C|\varepsilon||\alpha|^{2},\qquad 2\kappa^{-1}M\leq|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L,

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|c_{i}|,\qquad|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L,

and

\displaystyle|Err_{2}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|.

Proof.

We first show the estimates of
Err_{1}(Y)
. Recall that

\displaystyle Err_{1}(Y)=U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})\eta^{La}(\partial_{Y}\eta^{La})^{2}-\varepsilon\alpha^{2}-(U_{s}-c).

By the definition of
\eta^{La}(Y)
, we have

\displaystyle\eta^{La}(Y)=\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\eta_{in}^{La}(Y)+\Big(1-\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\Big)\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)-\mathrm{i}U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-1}c_{i},

which gives

\displaystyle Err_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\left(U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})(Y-Y_{c})(\partial_{Y}\eta^{La})^{2}-(U_{s}-c_{r})\right)

\displaystyle+\Big(1-\chi\Big(\frac{Y-Y_{c}}{\kappa^{-1}M}\Big)\Big)(U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)(\partial_{Y}\eta^{La})^{2}-(U_{s}-c_{r}))

\displaystyle+\mathrm{i}(1-(\partial_{Y}\eta^{La})^{2})c_{i}-\varepsilon\alpha^{2}.

Then by Lemma
4.1
and (
4.4
), we obtain that for
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq 2\kappa^{-1}M
,

(4.16)

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|Y-Y_{c}|^{2}+C|c_{i}||Y-Y_{c}|+C|\varepsilon||\alpha|^{2}.

For
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq 2\kappa^{-1}M
, we know that

\displaystyle\eta^{La}(Y)=\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)-\mathrm{i}U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{s})^{-1}c_{i},

which along with the fact
(\partial_{Y}\eta_{out}^{La}(Y))^{2}U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)=(U_{s}-c_{r})
implies that

\displaystyle Err_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})\eta_{out}^{La}(Y)\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}_{out}(Y)^{2}-(U_{s}-c_{r})+ic_{i}(1-\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)^{2})-\varepsilon\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}c_{i}(1-\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)^{2})-\varepsilon\alpha^{2}.

From Lemma
4.1
, we deduce that for any
Y\in\{Y\geq 0:|Y-Y_{c}|\geq 2\kappa^{-1}M,|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L\}
,

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|c_{i}||Y-Y_{c}|+C|\varepsilon||\alpha|^{2},

and for any
Y\in\{Y\geq 0:|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L\}
,

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|c_{i}|+C|\varepsilon||\alpha|^{2}\leq C|c_{i}|,

which along with (
4.16
) show that

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|(|c_{i}|+|\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|)+C|\varepsilon||\alpha|^{2},\qquad|Y-Y_{c}|\leq 2\kappa^{-1}M

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|c_{i}||\eta_{r}^{La}(Y)|+C|\varepsilon||\alpha|^{2},\qquad 2\kappa^{-1}M\leq|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L,

\displaystyle|Err_{1}(Y)|\leq C|c_{i}|,\qquad|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L.

Next we show the estimates of
Err_{2}(Y)
. Recall that
Err_{2}(Y)=\frac{\varepsilon\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta^{La}(Y)}{\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)}
. By Lemma
4.1
, we know that for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\varepsilon\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta^{La}(Y)}{\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)}\right|\leq C|\varepsilon|.

which implies that for any
|Y-Y_{c}|\leq L
,
|Err_{2}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|.
For
|Y-Y_{c}|\geq L
, by Lemma
4.1
again, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)|\geq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{1}{3}}\text{ and }|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta^{La}(Y)|\leq C(1+|Y-Y_{c}|)^{-\frac{4}{3}},

which implies

\displaystyle|Err_{2}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|.

∎

Finally, we show the estimates of the Green function of the approximate Airy equation (
4.11
).

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
0<\delta_{0}\ll 1\leq M
be the constants in Lemma
A.1
. Suppose
|\kappa\eta_{i}^{La}|<\delta_{0}
and
|\alpha|,|\varepsilon|,|c|\ll 1
. Then for any
0\leq Z\leq Y
, there exists
\gamma_{0}>0
such that for
k=0,1,2
and
j=0,1,2
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{k}

\displaystyle A_{1}(Y)A_{2}(j,Z)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2+k-j}{3}}\mathcal{M}(k,Y)\mathcal{M}(-j,Z)e^{-\gamma_{0}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta^{La}(Y)-\eta^{La}(Z)|(|\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\kappa\eta^{La}(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}})},

and

\displaystyle|\partial_{Y}^{k}

\displaystyle A_{2}(Z)A_{1}(j,Y)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2+k-j}{3}}\mathcal{M}(k,Z)\mathcal{M}(-j,Y)e^{-\gamma_{0}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta^{La}(Y)-\eta^{La}(Z)|(|\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\kappa\eta^{La}(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}})},

where
\mathcal{M}(l;Y)=|\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)|^{l}|\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)|^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{l}{2}}
for
Y\in\mathcal{N}^{+}\cup\mathcal{N}^{-}
and
\mathcal{M}(l;Y)=M^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{l}{2}}
for
Y\in\mathcal{N}
.

Proof.

By the definition of
A_{1}(Y)
and
A_{2}(Y)
, we notice that for any
Y\geq 0,
and
k=0,1
,

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}e^{-2\theta_{0}\mathrm{i}}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)\right)^{k}\partial_{Y}^{k}Ai\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{2}(Y)=

\displaystyle 2\pi\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{5\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)\right)^{k}\partial_{Y}^{k}Ai\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{5\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)\right),

and

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}^{2}A_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}e^{-2\theta_{0}\mathrm{i}}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{2}{3}}U_{s}^{\prime}(Y_{c})^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)\right)^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}Ai\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)\right)

\displaystyle+e^{-\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{3}+3\theta_{0})}|\varepsilon|^{-1}\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta^{La}(Y)\partial_{Y}Ai\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}^{2}A_{1}(Y)=

\displaystyle 2\pi\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{5\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)\right)^{2}\partial_{Y}^{2}Ai\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{5\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)\right)

\displaystyle+2\pi e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{5\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta^{La}(Y)\partial_{Y}Ai\left(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{5\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)\right).

Case 1.
Y,Z\in\mathcal{N}
and
0\leq Z\leq Y
.

By Lemma
A.4
and the properties of Airy functions, for any
0\leq Z\leq Y
and for
k=0,1
and
j=0,1,2
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{1}(Y)A_{2}(j,Z)\right|+\left|\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{2}(Z)A_{1}(j,Y)\right|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{j-2-k}{3}}|\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)|^{k}\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{j-2-k}{3}},

and

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{Y}^{2}A_{1}(Y)A_{2}(j,Z)\Big|+\Big|\partial_{Y}^{2}A_{2}(Z)A_{1}(j,Y)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{j-4}{3}}|\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)|^{2}+C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{j-3}{3}}|\partial_{Y}^{2}\eta^{La}(Y)|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{j-4}{3}}.

where we used the fact for any
Y\in\mathcal{N}
,
\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}\sim 1
. Hence, for any
k=0,1,2
and
j=0,1,2
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{1}(Y)A_{2}(j,Z)\right|+\left|\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{2}(Z)A_{1}(j,Y)\right|\leq C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{j-2-k}{3}}.

Case 2.
Y,Z\in\mathcal{N}^{+}\cup\mathcal{N}^{-}
and
0\leq Z\leq Y
.

According to (
A.1
), Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.4
, there exists
\gamma_{1}>0
such that for
k=0,1
and
j=0,1,2
,

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{1}(Y)A_{2}(j,Z)\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{j-2-k}{3}}\frac{|\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)|^{k}|\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)|^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{k}{2}}}{|\partial_{Z}\eta^{La}(Z)|^{j}|\kappa\eta^{La}(Z)|^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{j}{2}}}\Big|e^{-\frac{2}{3}((e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Y))^{\frac{3}{2}}-(e^{\mathrm{i}(\frac{5\pi}{6}-\theta_{0})}\kappa\eta^{La}(Z))^{\frac{3}{2}})}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\varepsilon|^{\frac{j-2-k}{3}}\frac{|\partial_{Y}\eta^{La}(Y)|^{k}|\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)|^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{k}{2}}}{|\partial_{Z}\eta^{La}(Z)|^{j}|\kappa\eta^{La}(Z)|^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{j}{2}}}e^{-\gamma_{1}|\varepsilon|^{-\frac{1}{3}}|\eta^{La}(Y)-\eta^{La}(Z)|(|\kappa\eta^{La}(Y)|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\kappa\eta^{La}(Z)|^{\frac{1}{2}})}.

Similarly, we can obtain that for any
Y,Z\in\mathcal{N}^{+}\cup\mathcal{N}^{-}
and
Z\leq Y
,

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{Y}^{k}A_{2}(Z)A_{1}(j,Y)\Big|
